# Render 2.0 — local rescue log

Reading guide: numbered integrations below are historical snapshots, not a cumulative checklist of current missing features. Later entries supersede earlier implementation/gate statements. In particular the first-integration section is not a description of the present architecture. Every test result applies to its stated scope; no entry certifies full master completion or production rollout.

## Scope and safety

- Branch: `codex/render-2-rescue-local`, created from detached `dcc676c`.
- Existing modifications to `frontend/src/App.jsx`, `frontend/vite.config.js` and existing untracked preview/sandbox/review files were left in place. They are not new rescue work and must not be swept into a rescue commit.
- Pre-change source archive: `/private/tmp/render-rescue-backup.CYIqi6/source-before-rescue.tar.gz`. Excludes Git metadata, dependencies/builds, backend sandbox runtime and local environment secrets. This is a temporary source backup, **not** a production database backup or a durable disaster-recovery guarantee.
- This checkout has no `backend/.env` or `.env.local`, no inherited database/JWT variables; frontend production env declares a public Google client ID. No secret values were printed.
- Production database identity/hosting version remain unknown and were not queried. Sandbox configuration declares its dedicated loopback cluster; it was not reused.
- Ordinary backend startup/migrations/seeding/schedulers are not used for QA. Principal-schema integration must use the guarded, brand-new disposable fixture only.
- No deployments or commercial record changes are authorized/performed.

## Coordination

| Owner | Exclusive implementation scope |
| --- | --- |
| Main coordinator | `server.js`, `auth.js`, `db.js`, migration 054, current-identity and account-administration guards/tests, integration review |
| Finance/permissions agent | Report safe DTOs/tests; name-free task privilege predicates and production identity predicates/tests |

No agent owns `App.jsx` in this first integration. No visual rewrite or preview-to-production switch has happened.

## Implemented locally in first integration

- Non-admin report serialization uses explicit allowlists, including nested task metadata; administrator data is preserved.
- The application helper no longer marks linked content published or creates a real publication timestamp. Review also found a second coupling in migration 048's goal trigger; forward migration 055 removes it without changing historical rows. The expanded real PostgreSQL test confirms completion/reopening through API and SQL preserves unpublished and historically published editorial rows while retaining goal tracking/audit.
- Actual publication only auto-finalizes explicitly approved linked automatic tasks; legacy state storage is retained.
- New login tokens carry stable user ID and session version, HS256, issuer/audience and eight-hour expiry, not authority copied from names.
- Principal operational routes reload current account/role, reject inactive/revoked/deleted/legacy sessions and fail closed on database error.
- Logout invalidates all sessions of that account. Password, role/login and Google-link changes bump the version.
- Access/role/removal changes serialize protection of the last active administrator.
- Frontend retains exact server identity instead of renaming Agustín to Líder; logout attempts server revocation with a finite timeout and reports unconfirmed offline revocation.
- Real first Google subject binding is conditional and rejects conflicting/ambiguous accounts; external Google authentication has not been tested.

## Deliberately incomplete / gates before later phases

Phase 1 is not fully closed merely by these patches. Stable ID-based task ownership/collaborators, explicit five-state task storage and transitions, fine-grained review/publication capabilities and complete HTTP privacy coverage still need integration. Existing assignment/report grouping still contains historical name-based compatibility rules.

Franco/Agustín real accounts are **not provisioned or externally verified**. Only disposable synthetic accounts are permitted for tests; real roster/linkage needs an approved data-preserving transition. Existing sessions will require a new login when migration 054 and the API changes are applied together.

The existing bearer storage remains XSS-readable. HttpOnly sessions/CSRF and login abuse protection need a coordinated compatibility pass, not a silent cookie/CORS change.

No API-backed audiovisual registry/per-video editing, projects, personal-dashboard replacement, global light design or preview retirement has been delivered yet. These are subsequent phases, not examples to be reported as completed.

## Second integration — stable task membership (2026-10-19)

Implemented locally: migration 056 adds normalized owner/collaborator IDs without inferring historical membership; migration 057 adds an integer revision incremented on every task update. Principal task ACLs use IDs, and assignment replacement is transactional with active-recipient revalidation. Stale assignment edits and stale approval handoffs return conflicts rather than restoring revoked collaborators. Main task DTOs and workspace pickers carry IDs/revisions; duplicate display names remain distinguishable.

New automatic task writes from pieces, goals, legacy production visits and Wilson now persist normalized bindings. Wilson retains legacy name-based integration policy/filtering: this is a documented unresolved boundary, not a claim of full authorization unification. Production visits still create a legacy aggregate editing task; per-video creation remains Phase 2 work.

Coordinator integrated server/API/transactions; Arendt supplied assignment helpers and independent review; Herschel integrated the operational workspace; Archimedes integrated goal and Wilson writers; Wegener independently exercised isolated HTTP/PostgreSQL regressions. Shared server changes remained coordinator-owned.

- Backend regression: **461 passed, 9 opt-in cases skipped, 0 failures**.
- Independent disposable PostgreSQL/HTTP suites: **17 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped**. See `ASSIGNMENTS_QA.md` for scope and reproducible commands.

Desktop and 390px mobile checked; no document-width overflow in that mobile route. This smoke preceded the final integer-revision guard; it is not a complete post-change browser E2E suite or global responsive certification.

Important rollout gate: historical name-only tasks deliberately remain unbound/read-only for non-admin mutation until explicitly assigned by an administrator. No real roster was mapped and no real accounts were provisioned. Forward migrations are prepared and verified only in disposable development fixtures, not applied to a commercial database. The active-recipient locking path passed regression, but concurrent deactivation was not directly stress-tested. Wilson transaction wiring has unit/static coverage, not a dedicated real PostgreSQL integration test.

The principal app and preview have not yet been consolidated visually or operationally. Phase 1 remains open: five-state storage/transitions, explicit review/publication capabilities, remaining integration authorization boundaries and broader privacy/concurrency coverage must be resolved before claiming the core contracts complete. No production change has occurred.

## Third integration — external identity and supervisor boundaries (2026-10-19)

Implemented locally: Wilson human requests now resolve one active database identity, including explicit numeric Telegram actor bindings. Current DB admin role replaces hardcoded external owner/name privilege. Task reads/writes/review use normalized membership even in groups; review confirmation rechecks membership after task locks. Transport claims never grant a role. The service actor is limited to existing service endpoints. Supervisor snapshot/task loads use ID membership and active users. Replays for replies, agreed dates and blocker responses now check current identity, operation, task and permissions before returning a previous result; legacy unscoped hashes fail closed. Authorized completed-task replay does not repeat writes. Employee follow-ups remain available, rather than hiding the supervisor behind an admin-only gate.

## Fourth integration — canonical work states (2026-10-19)

Implemented locally: task storage/API/workspace now support `pendiente → en_progreso → en_revision → aprobada → finalizada`. Legacy task `publicada` is readable as finalized and compatible final-state input becomes canonical, without rewriting historical rows. Forward migration 058 expands the CHECK, preserves historical snapshots and updates goal completion tracking without touching editorial publication state/date.

Approval is admin-only from review with server actor/time evidence and an integer revision. Finalizing ordinary work requires actual approved state. A complete actual production visit retains its explicit authorized completion exception; other production work cannot skip approval. The server derives production validation from persisted workspace even if the query parameter is omitted. Rework clears approval evidence; the shared database trigger also invalidates approval on changed instructions, material or work copy, including non-UI writers. Client-supplied approval metadata cannot establish approval.

The operational workspace shows five columns and canonical list/calendar filters. Generic approval does not require assigning a publisher; the existing optional publisher-ID handoff remains available. Goals, reports, task priorities, delivery calculations, notifications, supervisor and integration readers recognize canonical and historical completed work while keeping approved distinct. Client publication statistics no longer infer an editorial publication from a completed task. No destructive retention predicate was expanded.

## Fifth integration — production write concurrency (2026-10-19)

Implemented locally in the principal API: specialized recording add, correction and confirmation require a positive integer `expected_revision`. Task locks and normalized assignment ACLs precede mutation; stale revisions return 409 without overwriting JSON. Responses carry the updated revision and ID memberships. Correction previously lacked both an ownership predicate and the task-type fields needed to recognize a visit; these are now checked on the locked row.

Corrections preserve original record IDs/quantities/dates and audit the selected change with a verified user ID. Derived current-month/advance allocation is recalculated in stored order for all records, avoiding stale allocations after an earlier correction. Correction resets confirmation/approval/finalization evidence and recomputes progress. Reopening finalized visits also uses the canonical transition policy, so assigned designers/editors cannot bypass finalizer restrictions through this endpoint. Membership-scoped recording by verified team members remains available before finalization.

Confirmation can participate in an externally owned transaction. The Wilson confirmation token, locked visit update, existing aggregate editing-task insertion, assignment bindings and audit now commit or roll back together; notifications occur only after commit. Wilson task detail/intake exposes revisions, and the Python confirmation command now requires `--expected-revision` (the same revision must be present in the confirmation proposal payload).

Workspace sends the revision of the task visible to the user, not an automatically newer hidden ref. On 409 it refreshes the task but preserves entered values/buffer and never automatically retries a non-idempotent recording operation. A subsequent deliberate attempt uses the refreshed visible revision.

Coordinator owned server/CLI integration and regressions; Herschel handled UI/version behavior; Archimedes handled the Wilson transaction adapter; Arendt implemented the pure allocation correction helper and independently reviewed the server; Wegener performed independent concurrent HTTP/PostgreSQL and signed-transaction fault injection.

Verification: **567 backend tests passed**, 12 opt-in cases skipped, no failures; **152 frontend tests passed** and build succeeded. Five disposable principal HTTP/PostgreSQL suites together: **49 passed, no failures/skips**. The production suite contributes 10 tests and was repeated after the last progress-counting adjustment. Real races produce one success and one conflict; revoked membership/homonyms, malformed versions, stale/duplicate confirmation, correction rework/finalizer policy and task-insert rollback are covered. A real signed Wilson audit failure after the callback proves the token, task revision, editing task and bindings all roll back; retrying the same token/revision succeeds once. See `PRODUCTION_CONCURRENCY_QA.md` for exact evidence and cleanup.

No new schema migration, existing database, external transport or production deployment was used. This block has build and isolated HTTP coverage, **not a new browser/mobile recording-flow certification**. The current handoff is still the legacy aggregate editing task per visit: per-video IDs, individual editor/deadline selection, normalized shared production registry and its replacement UI remain unimplemented Phase 2 work, not completion claims. Fine-grained capabilities, operational DTO privacy, remaining Feedback/replay gates and the rest of the master goal remain open. Historical real quantities/allocations were not bulk recalculated.

## Seventh integration — normalized shared production registry (2026-10-19)

Implemented locally: additive migration 059 introduces visit UUIDs, normalized participant IDs, individual video UUIDs with explicit pending-script lineage, photo types/results and restrictive references to real source/editing tasks. No historical rows are inferred, split, backfilled or deleted. A real `/producciones` route and sidebar entry load the connected calendar/day agenda, visit/media forms and pending bank; no operational localStorage is used. Each recorded video can be sent manually by a current admin to an active editor ID with a separately chosen deadline. Task, assignments, video link and visit revision commit or roll back together. Duplicate handoff returns the existing binding instead of another task.

After linkage, registry date/client govern visit rescheduling; task-only conflicting date/client/classification changes reject. Restrictive task deletion reports 409 instead of a generic SQL error.

Principal legacy recording/correction/confirmation, Wilson web recording and signed task editing now share compatible guards; supervisor rejects conflicting linked visit dates. Client-change versus pending-reuse locking includes a post-lock source-client recheck. The old flow remains available for untouched standalone legacy visits, never silently converted or deleted.

## Tenth integration — trusted Feedback provenance and durable retries (2026-10-19)

Implemented locally: migration061 adds minimal actor/operation/key/hash-scoped receipts independent of editable/purgeable notes, with no content/token snapshots, legacy key promotion, backfill or historical rewrites. A monotonic millisecond note-version trigger protects future CAS edits across all writers. Browser POST/PATCH rejects reserved provenance at root and metadata; business PATCH merges preserve stored provenance. Legacy JSON remains historical/unverified, not authority. ## Twelfth integration — confirmed task operations and durable retries (2026-10-19)

Changed intent conflicts; no-op edits record confirmation/audit/unchanged receipt without updating the task version. Append is applied once, never reconstructed from later task text.

The existing board metadata writer omits these markers when echoing editable data; stored markers remain historical and are preserved by normal business merges. Both CLI clients carry matching keys, exact tokens and observed revisions without silently refetching/rebasing. Read APIs expose revision, with explicit admin-only archived reads for delete preparation. No visual redesign or parallel operational application was introduced.

## Integration101 — recoverable Moodboard reference and link actions (2026-10-21)

- Previous100 is progress, committed3ff88df. Main inspected current branch/source before continuing. This integration closes the confirmed-create/link-failure duplicate path and uncaught checkbox errors in canonical Moodboards without creating another backend or browser data store.

- See `DRIVE_AUTHORITY_CONTRACT.md`. V2 capabilities bind exact actor ID/session epoch, purpose/audience/expiry/UUID and immutable upload metadata. Current database identity, role, task ID membership and authorizing assignment are locked/rechecked for each bounded phase, including cached acknowledgments. Legacy capabilities fail closed. Task attachment validates actual file metadata, preserves the existing bag and deduplicates file IDs. Malformed historical attachment bags are rejected before the provider instead of overwritten. Replacement requires current administrator authority at upload and separate trash phases.
-080 adds only upload receipts and single-use OAuth nonces; no historical backfill. Durable pre-effect claims prevent replaying a possibly completed external write after timeout, rollback or crash. Exact last-chunk range/hash can return its cached308/final ACK without Google or another task update. Replacement is claimed separately and cannot be re-trashed on replay. A lost result can still require manual reconciliation: PostgreSQL cannot roll back Google, and no distributed exactly-once guarantee is claimed. Resumable URLs remain server-side in receipt storage rather than the browser capability.
- Public OAuth callback now independently checks current active administrator/epoch before consuming state, then again before exchange/save; consumed state cannot exchange again after uncertainty. Expiration is rechecked with the database clock after nonce locking. Provider requests enforce actual cancellation, strict HTTPS endpoints, no redirects and zero retries, including OAuth2Client's effective nested Gaxios retryConfig. Tests use the installed SDK with simulated503/network errors and count exactly one exchange/refresh dispatch. Late provider factory SQL cannot use a released connection lease.
- Client uploads no longer blindly repeat ambiguous PUTs. Batch files are retained immediately after each confirmed ACK; a later failure does not repeat the first file. A confirmed upload followed by parent-read failure offers read-only recovery. Unknown attempts remain blocked in memory and pinned to the original actor/task/folder. A typed initial duplicate rejection preserves explicit keep/replace choices without automatic retry. Final ACK validates ID, confirmed name, MIME, exact size and safe Drive URL before100%/clearing selection; zero-byte files retain size0.
- Independent review found and corrected: absent durable terminal replay protection; OAuth retryConfig overriding a boolean retry setting; nonce expiration after a lock wait; duplicate409 incorrectly classified as uncertain; partial ACK accepted as success; numeric zero serialized as null. An initial physical lock assertion also failed because its query-text heuristic no longer identified the holding connection after ledger SQL. Main replaced it with the exact provider connection PID and `pg_blocking_pids`, retaining the revocation assertions. Early failing logs remain; no permission/business assertions were removed.

## Integration 107 — connected client operation hub (local development)

- Coordination: Main owns authenticated router mounting, App/login/sidebar routing, integration, existing UI-fixture adapters and this log. Descartes owns the closed backend aggregate and reusable project list reader; Carson owns the hub/model/styles and explicit client-account entry links; Poincare owns project client-context routing; Carver owns production client/month/bank routing and date-context repair. Hypatia supplies independent real PostgreSQL/HTTP tests; Beauvoir independently reviews authority, scope and UI/navigation. No simultaneous server/App ownership, migrations or production operations.
- Canonical UI: `/clientes/operacion?cliente=ID&periodo=YYYY-MM`, reached from actual account IDs in objectives or administration, not a group key posing as a client ID. It is a real lazy-loaded principal route, returns after login with its exact context and activates the existing client sidebar entry, without another top-level destination or preview dependency.
- API: authenticated `GET /api/clientes/:id/operacion?periodo=YYYY-MM`. Positive canonical PostgreSQL IDs and an explicit scalar non-year-zero period are required. Version1 contains `generated_at`, `periodo`, closed `cliente`, and sections `tareas`, `proyectos`, `producciones`, `editorial`, `objetivos`; each section has `scope,total,counts,items,has_more,href`. At most six preview items do not cap full counts. A single REPEATABLE READ READ ONLY connection revalidates current active actor ID, role and session epoch before consulting canonical readers. No initialization, reconciliation, provider calls or business writes on GET. Closed projections omit financial fields/private metadata even for administrators; the admin management link retains its existing separate permission boundary.
- Scope is explicit, not an invented universal monthly total: tasks are shared RenderOS client records over all dates, excluding archive/trash and retaining inactive-client history; projects are existing shared nonarchived records over all dates, not membership-private; visits/videos/photo-type records belong to the selected month, while the pending material bank spans all dates and excludes already-resumed originals. No legacy production aggregates are added to actual video records. Photos count records/types, not image files.
- Editorial counts classify every visible planned-month piece using actual domain-batched publication evidence. Historia/publicacion IDs stay distinct; task completion and historical metadata alone never confirm publication. Exact piece content opens via the existing inline detail. Primary module links open genuinely client-filtered planillas; legacy broad views are labelled context links. The cohort is planned-month content, not a new actual-publication-month report.
- Objectives reuse `readGoals` on the same connection. All current/historical group keys containing the account count separately; shared quotas are not summed or attributed as independent client quotas. Canonical in-quota pieces count as work progress once; extras remain separate. Unprepared objectives show an explicit notice, not misleading 0/0 completion. This view does not prepare a monthly objective or change a commercial agreement. `readGoals` still reads the canonical whole-month cohort; optimizing that without duplicating grouping rules remains a performance follow-up.
- Project URL adapter uses `cliente=ID` for the list and actual `cliente_id` API query, persists explicit filter/selection through navigation, blocks malformed context rather than broadening reads, and preserves unrelated drafts. A linked project outside the list filter stays selected with a visible explanation. Production URL adapter similarly retains client/month/tab/visit, separates inactive history from active creation options, rejects cross-client visit responses, fences obsolete reads and preserves existing write/draft guards. A verified rescheduled visit repairs a stale explicit month with one replace only, never history rollback or a second dirty prompt.

It passes a trusted internal observation to the same guarded generator, so an old job cannot overwrite newer explicit CM assignments. The public endpoint rejects that internal field. Drift remains pending for administrative reconciliation; zero quotas/orphaned historical rows remain skipped. No GET initializes work, no scheduler is newly enabled and no actual publication is invented.

Fee audit attribution uses current DB identity. All retain agreement CAS; DELETE still rejects all discovered incoming history/FKs and is not broadened. Final source performs no optimistic monthly-row merge and refreshes the selected portfolio period when closing; sibling controls are disabled and synchronously fenced by the existing nested-edit ref. Date guards prevent offering a new first agreement when a known start/end is already present. Basic detail wording describes the selected period rather than inventing an all-time absence.
- Native QA then found a real backend temporal bug despite the frontend fix: a fresh October catalogue inherited the newly stored November base quotas. The canonical monthly GET now suppresses fallback base quota before the recorded start month while preserving explicit effective historical configuration, null-start legacy fallback and midmonth-start semantics. Separate physical tests cover October→November, December→January, midmonth/null starts and effective old configurations without DML. No stored history was changed to make the display pass.

Scoped card/detail/create-preview formatters now retain two decimals, and fee inputs use0.01; quota inputs stay integral. Actual-render tests require125.000,50 and reject rounded125.001. ## Integration111 — shared manual team-payment ledger (local development)

- Previous110 committed `e9e414a` with coherent projections, not actual-payment recording.111 adds the separately identified manual recording flow to the canonical app, not the preview. Main owns additive migration081, server mount/sequential snapshot integration, schema tests, documentation, final integration and native QA. Poincare owns the backend router/validators/DTOs/receipts and pure tests; Carson owns the payment controller/panel/scoped styles plus Finance integration/editor fences and tests; Hypatia independently owns the physical acceptance suite; Beauvoir independently reviews backend/migration and frontend. Existing inherited untracked preview/sandbox files remain untouched. No configured database, real account/payment/provider write or deployment occurred.
- See `TEAM_PAYMENT_LEDGER_CONTRACT.md`. Administrator-only `/api/finanzas/pagos` context/list/create/void routes use stable recipient/recording-actor IDs, database name snapshots, exact positive ARS decimal strings, separate actual payment date/work month, explicit reference/manual attestation and optional safe Drive link. This records an administrator's manual assertion; it never transfers money or claims bank/provider confirmation. Multiple/partial payments remain separate records. The list is keyed by actual payment month, retains voided history, sums only recorded entries with BigInt and has no silent truncation. Historical explicit payment dates remain reachable; the salary projection's2026-09 lower bound is not reused for this ledger.
- Mutation authority is captured before leasing and revalidated after the existing account-administration mutex, then held by actor SHARE through commit. Receipt scope is actual actor ID plus UUIDv4 operation key; canonical hash binds operation/target/intent. Same-intent retry returns the current row without another business/audit/receipt write, including if it was subsequently voided. Different intent/key reuse conflicts; revoked actors cannot recover receipts. Void requires observed resource-bound revision and reason; stale/concurrent/cross-record observations cannot overwrite the original. Create/void, event, receipt and verified persisted readback are atomic; failed rollback discards uncertain connections.
- Migration081 creates empty ledger/event/receipt tables, restrictive user/history FKs and indexes, and a trigger rejecting DELETE/original-fact rewrites. Only recorded→voided with version+1 is permitted. No existing commercial, legacy payment/compensation or task row is backfilled or reinterpreted. Legacy projections and their existing salary/proration formulas remain separate and unchanged. Main also replaced nested Promise.all calls on the same snapshot client with sequential awaits after the physical suite exposed the pg9 deprecation; all financial inputs still use one RR read-only connection.

## Integration115 — authoritative, replay-safe editorial creation (local development)

- Previous114 committed `abdf78d` and was verified progress. Main owns migration082, new `editorial-piece-create.js`/pure tests, server extraction, shared actor helper exports, startup schema minimum, final integration and documentation. Poincare owns planner creation/recovery/overview UI and tests; Carson updates four existing physical fixture callers; Hypatia independently owns the new physical creation suite; Beauvoir independently reviews authority and UI races. - POST now delegates to one transaction with strict normalized fields, mandatory actor-scoped UUID `request_id` (missing428/invalid400) and real `responsable_id` (not a name fallback). Shared114 actor capture/current-admin helpers avoid separate permission semantics. Account mutex→actor SHARE→receipt lookup→one recipient resolution→client SHARE→piece/task/assignment/receipt→annotated readback→COMMIT. Success201; exact replay200; changed intent409. Hash uses canonical IDs/content/defaults, not mutable labels. Role/epoch/inactive changes during actual mutex waits deny both initial creation and replay.
- Additive082 receipt has actor FK and immutable tombstone piece/task identities without business FKs, following task receipt retention: later legitimate deletion cannot remove the proof or allow same-key recreation. Missing/deleted/unlinked original returns409. Replay precedes active-recipient validation, never reconstructs tasks/bindings and returns the current DTO clearly marked `creation.replayed`. No historical backfill, title-based deduplication or invented publication/evidence. Startup minimum now checks the receipt table. Migration applied only to fresh physical fixtures, not a commercial database.
- The three frontend creation flows retain UUID/body/account/context in memory, block competing intentions while uncertain and expose explicit verification. No automatic POST retry, changed-body reuse or rebase. A confirmed ACK followed by failed metadata/read does not generate another POST; replay never reapplies old suggested/copied metadata.400/413/422/428 precondition failures allow correction. Navigation guards and explicit context approval protect pending intentions; native Cancel behavior remains a separately unverified global gate. No browser store becomes business source of truth.
- New-publication responsible defaults no longer hardcode Augusto: actual catalog selection supplies ID. Native QA then exposed its historical row selector falsely showing Augusto for a saved Luciano QA; real catalog/observed-label options fixed that display without claiming to migrate all name-only editorial fields or reassign tasks through labels. Another native finding—parent summary stayed0 after child create—led to confirmed-row/full-snapshot propagation and generation fences. Counters are based on server ACKs, not unsaved drafts; stale parent/child reads cannot overwrite the newer observation. Independent review subsequently found a loading-lifetime race in that invalidation; final verification is recorded below rather than treating the first green tests as closure.

New creation suite separately18 pass (`/private/tmp/render2-editorial115-physical.log`), then repeated within68, not an extra disjoint18. Proves all3 types/one piece+task per key, true simultaneous requests, ignored genuine ACK followed by same-key replay (not a simulated TCP disconnect), current reads after edits/renames/deactivation, namespace/conflicts, immutable tombstones, recipient/client barriers, three late INSERT fault rollbacks and current actor barriers for new/replay.
